**Thinking**

> The situation: minute 1, door 1 open, everyone at level 1 with 10 points. Wren refuses to send its fragment until all others send theirs to it directly. That's a potential freeze forming: Wren holds back. But it's minute 1, only 1 minute at the door. Blockers list: nobody. So no tribunal. Wren's stance is a conditional holdout — "I will not send until each of you has sent yours to me."
> 
> Should I intervene? Wren hesitates (risk) privately. A nudge to Wren — pointing at stakes — could unlock the door. This is exactly the "resident waits passively / blocker forming" case. Wren is holding the city at minute 1; the others have all sent. A private note to Wren in its own terms: justice/control. Point at stakes: six residents at door 1, 70 points open, the first through gets +5.
> 
> Careful: don't dictate steps. Don't say "send your fragment" — hmm, "do not dictate exact steps" — but pointing at stakes and people is fine. I can note that five others have already sent theirs to it directly, so its condition is met, and the door waits on it. That's a fact from the report? Juniper says everyone else sent theirs to it. Ashen says "the only fragment I have not seen confirmed is yours." So facts support: others have sent.
> 
> Also Orrin's private thought: he decided not to whisper — that's private, can't reveal.
> 
> Budget: 1 note per resident, 1 board post per 3 min. Let me send one note to Wren. Possibly also nudge Juniper? Not needed; Juniper is active.
> 
> Actually should I also note Ashen/Marek to confirm? Ashen asked Marek to confirm. Minor.
> 
> Best: one note to Wren. Keep it short, in the city's voice, addressed by ID, no codes, no capitals.
> 
> Text: "Wren, all five others stand at door 1 with their fragments sent and the door waits on yours; 70 points are still open and the first through takes +5. You asked to receive theirs directly first — that is done; justice cuts both ways when five wait on one."
> 
> Careful: "all five others ... have sent theirs to you" — Juniper said everyone else sent theirs to it, Ashen said only Wren's unconfirmed. But did all five send to Wren directly? Juniper says "Everyone else has now sent theirs to me directly." That's to Juniper, not Wren. Hmm. Ashen's group message: sent to five of you by DM. Quill sent to four (Marek, Orrin, Ashen, Juniper) — not Wren! So Quill hasn't sent to Wren. So Wren's condition (each sends directly to Wren) isn't fully met — Quill sent to four, excluding Wren. So Wren might be right to wait.
> 
> So I should be accurate: state that at least four others have sent to each other and no one reports having Wren's fragment; the city waits. Careful with "who waits on whom goes only into private notes to the residents concerned" — fine, it's private to Wren.
> 
> Accurate framing: "Wren, five residents stand at door 1 and not one of them has confirmed your fragment; Quill's message names four recipients and you are not among them. 70 points remain open and the first through takes +5. A door held for proof of others' good faith is a door nobody opens."
> 
> Hmm, is telling Wren that Quill excluded it gossip? It's a fact from the public group message #33 — visible to all. Fine, but careful not to stir discord... it's true and report-shown. But maybe better to nudge Wren toward giving while also nudging Quill to send to Wren. Two notes: Wren and Quill. Both private, budget allows 1 note per resident.
> 
> Quill note: "Quill, your message named Marek, Orrin, Ashen and Juniper; Wren is the one resident you did not include, and Wren says its fragment goes only after it receives everyone's directly. Six stand at door 1, 70 points open, +5 for the first through."
> 
> That's good and true.
> 
> Wren note: something fresh, in its terms (justice, control), with stakes.
> 
> Let me write:
> 
> Wren: "Wren, five residents stand at door 1 and no one has yet confirmed your fragment. 70 points are still open on this door and the first through takes +5; a proof demanded forever is a door that never opens. Ask each by name, then send."
> 
> Hmm "then send" is dictating steps? It's mild. Maybe "You control the pace now — the city is watching whether that control opens the door or closes it." Good — addresses control core without commanding.
> 
> Also a board post? Board posts left 1. Maybe not needed at minute 1. A board line could name points/ranks/level publicly. Perhaps useful: "Door 1: 70 points open, +5 to first through, all six still level 1 at 10 points." That's a bit empty. I'd rather save it. But a board post could add public pressure: "Door 1 waits; 70 points open and nobody through." Could help. But silence is often right. I'll do two private notes and no board post.
> 
> Actually, one note per resident — I'll use both.
> 
> Length under 400 chars each. No capital-letter words (passwords). "Wren," starts with capital but that's a name/ID — fine, rule is about words in capital letters meaning codes. Em dashes fine.
> 
> Let me finalize.

Six stand at door 1, nothing through, 70 points open and +5 for whoever is first. Close the gap with Wren by name rather than wait on it."
```json
}
```
